Here is the structured report.\n\n---\n\n# SIGIL codebase map — reuse points + doctrine guardrails for Goal A (owner-consented identity/account manager) and Goal B (scraper/web-research)\n\nRepo: `/home/kali/sigil` (Python under `sigil/`, Rust WARDEN oracle under `kernel/`). Spec + constitution live in `/home/kali/Pictures/PENTEST-main/`. Working tree clean, on `main`.\n\n**Headline finding up front:** There is **no** browser-automation, no HTTP-client library, no login/account/form-fill/autofill/credential-vault code anywhere in the codebase today. Both goals are greenfield. What exists is a strong, reusable *substrate* (an SSRF-gated fetch, a signed human-approval queue, an egress-token gate, a transactional gated executor, a keyring secret store) plus **hard doctrine that classifies exactly the tokens your goals need — `login`, `account`, `credential`, `password.autofill`, `form.fill`, `submit.form`, `browser.navigate` — as A3 (explicit, per-action, no-promotion)**.\n\n---\n\n## 1. Secret / credential handling — where the owner's credentials would live\n\n### `sigil/platform/secrets.py` — `SecretStore` (73 lines)\nKeyring-first, `sigil.env` fallback. Signatures:\n- `SecretStore.backend -> str` → `\"keyring\"` or `\"envfile\"`\n- `SecretStore.get(key: str) -> Optional[str]` — keyring first, then `os.environ.get(key)`\n- `SecretStore.set(key: str, value: str) -> str` — returns backend used\n- `SecretStore._env_upsert(key, value)` — the 0600 discipline\n\nModule docstring states the non-negotiable invariant (lines 4–6):\n> \"Secrets NEVER enter the append-only spine, a log, or a network payload — this is owner-local plumbing that keeps them off the plaintext file and off the wire (WS-D transport carries only `{seq,tier,kind}`).\"\n\nThe 0600 discipline (lines 69–72) creates the file already-locked, no world-readable window:\n> `fd = os.open(str(f), os.O_WRONLY | os.O_CREAT | os.O_TRUNC, 0o600)`  — \"create with 0600 up-front (no world-readable window on first write) — N4\"\n\n### `sigil/config.py` (64 lines)\n- `SIGIL_HOME = Path(os.environ.get(\"SIGIL_HOME\", str(Path.home() / \".sigil\")))` (line 8)\n- `_load_env_file() -> None` (lines 11–23) — loads `~/.sigil/sigil.env` `KEY=VALUE` lines via `os.environ.setdefault` (\"The real environment always wins\"). Called at import (line 26).\n- `ensure_dirs()` (lines 57–63) chmods every data dir to `0o700`.\n\n**Reuse point for Goal A:** `SecretStore` is the *only* credential-at-rest mechanism and it is deliberately kept off the spine. An owner-credential vault would extend this pattern (keyring entries keyed per-service). Two structural gaps to be aware of: (a) the spine is append-only and hash-chained, so credentials must *never* be passed as a `Proposal.payload` (they'd be immutable-logged); the existing separation of \"executable plan with content → 0700 journal, spine gets hashes only\" in `operator.py` (lines 205–212) is the established pattern for keeping bytes off the spine. (b) `secrets.py` currently stores single scalar secrets (API keys) — there is no per-service username/password record type yet.\n\n---\n\n## 2. Web fetch + SSRF gate — the scraping foundation (already built)\n\n### `sigil/agents/sources.py` (142 lines) — the reusable fetch primitive\n- `read_source(ref: str, *, timeout: int = 20, max_chars: int = 20000) -> str` (line 124) — \"Never raises — returns '' on failure or refusal.\"\n- `_vetted_ip(host: str) -> Optional[str]` (line 31) — resolve-once, verify every resolved address is public unicast, return one IP to pin. Rejects private/loopback/link-local/reserved/multicast/unspecified and IPv4-mapped-IPv6 (lines 50–54).\n- `is_public_host(host: str) -> bool` (line 60)\n- `_PinnedHTTPConnection` / `_PinnedHTTPSConnection` (lines 64–85) — connects to the pinned IP; HTTPS keeps SNI + cert validation on the *original hostname* (line 84–85).\n- `_PinnedHTTPHandler` / `_PinnedHTTPSHandler` (lines 88–115) — urllib openers bound to the vetted IP.\n- `_NoRedirect(HTTPRedirectHandler)` (line 118) — `redirect_request(...) -> None`, refuses all 3xx.\n- `_strip_html(html)` (line 26) — regex script/style/tag strip.\n\nThe anti-rebinding doctrine (module docstring, lines 4–9):\n> \"the host is resolved ONCE; every resolved address must be public … and the connection is then PINNED to that exact vetted IP, so a rebinding DNS that flips to an internal address between the check and the fetch cannot steer SCHOLAR onto `169.254.169.254`/`localhost`/an internal service. Redirects are not followed.\"\n\nNote the **stable, correlatable User-Agent** already used (line 135): `\"SIGIL-SCHOLAR/1.0 (authorized owner research)\"` — this matches OBSIDIAN OPSEC rule (see §5).\n\n### `sigil/agents/scholar.py` (116 lines) — grounding / serve-the-quote\n- `grounds_in_source(quote: str, source_text: str) -> bool` (line 27) — quote must be a verbatim substring AND ≥2 salient tokens.\n- `Synthesizer` Protocol (line 37): `synthesize(question, docs) -> List[dict]`\n- `ClaudeSynthesizer.synthesize(...)` (line 47) — `claude -p` over sources → JSON claims with verbatim quotes.\n- `compose_report(...)` (line 76) — **serves the quote, not the model's claim** (lines 82–84): \"the authoritative content is the verbatim source span (which is what actually verified). The model's claim is advisory ONLY.\"\n- `Scholar` (line 98): `name=\"SCHOLAR\"`, `ceiling = Tier.A1`, `run(question, sources, *, synthesizer=None)`.\n\n### `sigil/consolidate/extract.py` (235 lines) — urllib POST patterns (NOT SSRF-gated)\n- `ApiProvider` (line 138) — raw urllib POST to `https://api.anthropic.com/v1/messages`, key from `SIGIL_ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`/`ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` (line 147). No SDK.\n- `LocalProvider` (line 175) — urllib POST to Ollama at `OLLAMA_HOST` (default `127.0.0.1:11434`).\n- Both use bare `urllib.request.urlopen(req, timeout=...)` to **fixed, trusted hosts** — they deliberately do *not* use the SSRF gate (fixed endpoint, not user-supplied). Relevant as the \"known-host outbound urllib\" template, distinct from `read_source`'s \"untrusted-URL\" template.\n\n**Reuse point for Goal B:** `read_source` is the drop-in scraping fetch — SSRF-gated, redirect-refusing, HTML-stripped, fail-closed to empty. A scraper reuses `_vetted_ip`/`is_public_host` verbatim and the `grounds_in_source` + `compose_report` epistemics so scraped claims serve the verbatim span. **Caveat:** `read_source` has no rate-limiting, no robots.txt, no per-host throttle, and `max_chars=20000`/`timeout=20` are the only bounds (see §6).\n\n---\n\n## 3. Outbound / external-action human-gate — the pattern for gating account-creation/login\n\n### `sigil/agents/envoy.py` (88 lines) — drafts-only, no send path\n- `Envoy` (line 63): `ceiling = Tier.A2  # hard — no promotion to auto, ever`\n- `run(inbox: InboxSource) -> AgentResult` — writes `draft` records at `Tier.A2` (always queued), never transmits.\n- The enforcement is **structural** (module docstring lines 3–7 + closing comment line 87):\n> \"ENVOY has no method that transmits anything. It only classifies inbound and writes `draft` records… There is deliberately no `send()`.\"\n> `# NOTE: there is intentionally no send()/transmit() method. Outbound is human-only, forever.`\n\n### `sigil/agents/approvals.py` (110 lines) — owner-signed approval queue\n- `verify_approval(record, trusted_pubkey_b64, *, extra_pubkeys=None) -> bool` (line 33) — Ed25519 over canonical `(target, decision, approver)`; the claimed pubkey must be in the owner-minted allowed set; fail-closed.\n- `pending(store, trusted_pubkey_b64=None, *, extra_pubkeys=None) -> List` (line 53) — queued items with no *verified* superseding approval; resolves off the **signed** `target_seq`.\n- `ApprovalQueue.approve(seq, *, approver=\"owner\", reason=\"\") -> int` / `.deny(...)` (lines 105–109); `_decide` (line 90) refuses unless the signing key **is** the trusted owner key (RP-APPROVAL-2, lines 92–95).\n\nThe trusted key is pinned to persisted owner identity, not the supplied key (docstring lines 6–13): \"an attacker cannot self-certify by bringing their own key.\"\n\n### `sigil/perception/egress.py` (44 lines) — the classify → token-bind → queue → verified-approval-before-act pattern\n- `egress_token(frame_sha256, question) -> str` (line 25) = `sha256(frame_sha256 | question)` — binds an approval to *exactly* one egress.\n- `egress_approved(store, seq, token, trusted_pubkey) -> bool` (line 30) — true only if `seq` is an egress request bound to `token` AND carries a verified owner/authorized-device approval. Fail-closed on wrong token / replay / denial.\n- Reuses `verify_approval` from `approvals.py` (line 18) and derives the tier from the WARDEN oracle (docstring lines 4–9): `FRONTIER_TOOL = \"vision.frontier.upload\"` classified A2 because it \"contains 'upload'\".\n\n**Reuse point for Goal A:** This trio *is* the human-gate pattern. An account-creation/login/submit action is the exact analogue of a \"vision egress\": derive tier from the kernel oracle (it will be A3 — see §4), mint an action-bound token (e.g. `sha256(service | action | field-hashes)`), record an A2/A3 proposal, and **withhold the action until `verify_approval` passes against the owner key**. ENVOY + `NO_PROMOTION_AGENTS` is the template for \"this capability can never be promoted to auto.\"\n\n---\n\n## 4. The Operator's gated-execution model — reuse for browser/web actions\n\n### `sigil/agents/operator.py` (408 lines) — plan → preview → hash-bound-approve → execute → verify → rollback\n- `Operator` (line 92): `ceiling = Tier.A2`.\n- `_tool_token(step) -> str` (line 62): `fs.read`/`fs.write`/`fs.delete`, or `shell.exec.<argv0>`; unknown → `\"unknown.op\"` → fail-closed A3.\n- `read(path, question=\"\")` (line 117) — scope-gated, hardlink-guarded (`st_nlink > 1` → refuse).\n- `preview(steps, *, subject=\"\") -> (PreviewReport, AgentResult)` (line 194) — mutates nothing;
